<a id="finance-ops"></a>

## 31. Entity, GST, accounting and cash controls

### 31.1 First decision: can you lawfully run the side business?

Before outreach or use of any work product, review your employment agreement and HR policies. Check the rules on confidentiality, IP assignment, non-solicitation, moonlighting, conflicts of interest, devices and data. Get written clarification when needed. Never use your employer’s time, laptop, software, code, credentials, prospect or customer data, staff or reputation. If your employer and target client work in related markets, get specific advice on Indian employment law.

### 31.2 Entity path

| Structure | Useful when | Advantages | Costs/risks | Starting recommendation |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Individual/sole proprietorship | One founder is testing a service | Fast to start, little administration and direct control | No separate limited liability. The founder and business are legally tied together. Some clients or procurement teams may reject it | Usually the simplest pilot-stage choice after a CA and lawyer confirm it |
| Partnership | Two or more founders | A simple shared-business structure | Partners may face broad liability. Disputes and succession can be hard | If two founders need limited liability, usually prefer a properly drafted LLP |
| LLP | Several active owners, larger contracts or a need for clearer separation | A separate legal entity, limited liability subject to law and a flexible agreement | Incorporation, annual filings, compliance work and professional fees | Consider it after proof, a co-founder or a contract-risk trigger |
| Private limited company | Equity investors, ESOPs or a larger product and company plan | A familiar structure for fundraising and governance, with legal separation | The largest ongoing load for governance, filings, payroll, the board and administration | You do not need it for interviews or the first narrow service pilots |

Limited liability does not protect fraud, personal guarantees, professional negligence or every legal risk. Choose a structure based on contracts, tax, co-founders and risk. Do not choose it for status.

### 31.3 Upgrade triggers

Review the entity choice again when you add a co-founder or equity, make major hires, or sign contracts with high liability, enterprise clients or cross-border work. Also review it before taking outside investment or debt, building a product or IP to license, or bidding where a certain entity is required. Review it when revenue, tax or procurement makes the current form inefficient.

### 31.4 Registration and professional-advice checklist

| Item | When to assess | Owner/source |
|---|---|---|
| PAN and tax return treatment | Before the first receipt or expense | Ask a CA and check the Income Tax portal |
| Dedicated current/business bank account | Before regular client collections start | Ask the bank and CA. Keep business money separate |
| GST registration | Before liability starts, a voluntary choice or a contract quote | Ask a CA and check current CBIC/GST portal rules |
| Udyam/MSME | When real enterprise details exist | Use the official Udyam portal. Registration is currently free, paperless and based on self-declaration |
| Maharashtra PTEC | At the start or when the entity or profession status requires it | Ask a CA and the Maharashtra GST Department |
| PTRC/payroll duties | Before hiring people, where these duties apply | Ask a payroll adviser and the Maharashtra GST Department |
| Maharashtra Shops and Establishments | Based on the real workplace and worker count | Ask a labour adviser and check the Maharashtra Labour Management System |
| TAN/TDS | Before a transaction or payroll that requires deduction | Ask a CA |
| Import/export/cross-border tax | Before money flows to or from a foreign customer or vendor | Ask a CA, GST/FEMA adviser and bank |
| Local professional/trade permissions | As required by the office and activity | Ask a Pune or local adviser |
| Trademark/domain | After checking the name and making a real brand commitment | Ask an IP professional. A trademark is not company or GST registration |
| RERA role analysis | Before any activity that looks like facilitation, brokerage or commission | Ask a Maharashtra real-estate lawyer |
| Insurance | Before high-risk or enterprise production work | Ask a broker or lawyer about professional indemnity and cyber options |

Use only the government [Udyam portal](https://www.udyamregistration.gov.in/default.aspx). It says registration is free, paperless, based on self-declaration and does not need renewal. When accessed on 3 September 2026, it classified a micro-enterprise as having investment not above ₹2.5 crore **and** turnover not above ₹10 crore. This rule took effect on 1 April 2025. Udyam registration does not replace GST, tax, labour, local or sector-specific compliance.

### 31.5 GST decision control

For a service supplier in Maharashtra, the general GST registration threshold is ₹20 lakh of aggregate turnover in one financial year. Calculate it across India for all persons with the same PAN. Aggregate turnover includes taxable, exempt, export and inter-State supplies. It does not include GST or inward supplies where tax is paid under reverse charge. Notification 10/2017–Integrated Tax removes compulsory registration for qualifying inter-State taxable service suppliers below the applicable threshold. Apply within the legal period, which is generally 30 days after you become liable. Ask the CA to check every compulsory-registration exception. Also check voluntary registration, place of supply, exports, reverse charge and platform facts separately. See [Notification 10/2017–Integrated Tax](https://cbic-gst.gov.in/hindi/pdf/integrated-tax/10_2017_IT.pdf) and the [GST registration rules](https://cbic-gst.gov.in/gst-registration-rules.html).

Follow this review flow:

```text
Identify the legal supplier and PAN
→ map every supply, client and location, and calculate aggregate turnover
→ classify the service and place of supply
→ test the threshold and rules or exemptions for compulsory registration
→ assess the cost and benefit of voluntary registration and the client’s ITC expectations
→ record the CA’s conclusion, effective date and invoice treatment
→ check cumulative turnover each month and review again after any change
```

Do not charge “GST” without valid registration and authority. After voluntary or mandatory registration, follow all applicable rules for invoices, returns, payment, records and reconciliation from the correct effective date.

### 31.6 GST commercial design

- Quote `professional fee + applicable taxes`. Do not accidentally promise a margin that already includes tax.
- Keep your fee separate from the client’s software, message, ad, telephony and cloud costs.
- For reimbursed or pass-through costs, ask the CA whether you act as principal, agent or claim pure-agent treatment. The label alone does not decide the tax.
- Before invoicing, record the client’s legal name, address, GSTIN or status and place-of-supply facts.
- Match the sales ledger, invoices, credit notes, bank receipts and GST returns.
- Never use another firm’s GSTIN. Never split invoices or entities to avoid thresholds.
- For a foreign client, check export-of-service and place-of-supply rules, how currency is received, LUT or refund rules, FEMA and bank rules, and permanent-establishment effects. Do this before you quote “zero-rated.”
- Buying foreign SaaS or APIs may count as imported services under IGST reverse charge. Get the CA’s treatment before purchase.
- Foreign-client work is a zero-rated export only when every export-of-service condition is met. Introducing or helping leads may create GST “intermediary” place-of-supply risk even if the contract calls the work consulting. Review [CBIC Circular 159/15/2021-GST](https://cbic-gst.gov.in/pdf/Circular-No-159-14-2021-GST.pdf).
- Income-tax section 44AD does not cover agency, commission or brokerage income. Do not assume presumptive taxation applies to a RERA or success-fee model. Review the [Income Tax Department ITR-4/44AD guidance](https://www.incometax.gov.in/iec/foportal/help/e-filing-itr4-form-sugam-faq?mobile-app=1) with the CA.
- GST e-invoicing currently applies if aggregate annual turnover reached ₹5 crore or more in any financial year from 2017–18 onward. This rule took effect on 1 August 2023. Set up controls before reaching that scale, and check the current threshold on the [GSTN e-invoice mandate page](https://einvoice6.gst.gov.in/content/einvoice-mandate/).

### 31.7 Invoice checklist

Ask the CA to approve a template that includes these items when applicable:

- A unique invoice number in sequence, plus the date;
- The supplier’s legal and trade names, address, PAN/GSTIN and contact;
- The client’s legal name, address, GSTIN or status and place-of-supply details;
- The contract, SOW and PO, plus the service period or milestone;
- A correct service description and the advised classification or SAC;
- The taxable value, discounts and tax rate, type and amount when applicable;
- The total in numbers and words, plus the currency;
- Payment terms, due date and bank details;
- The required reverse-charge, export, LUT or other statement where applicable;
- The authorised signatory or digital method;
- A credit-note reference for corrections. Never quietly edit an invoice after issue.

### 31.8 Contract document set

| Document | Purpose |
|---|---|
| Mutual NDA | Protect confidential information shared before the contract. It does not replace data-processing terms |
| Master services agreement | Set the general legal and commercial relationship |
| Statement of work | State the exact project, limits, deliverables, tests, fee and timeline |
| Data-processing/privacy schedule | State roles, instructions, fields, vendors, security, rights, incidents and data return or deletion |
| Security schedule | Set access, the minimum controls, audit evidence and duties |
| SLA/support schedule | Set hours, severity, response and update times, dependencies and remedies |
| Change order | Cover a new project, source, channel, feature, volume or support need and its price effect |
| Acceptance certificate | Record test evidence, known limits and approval to launch |
| Case-study consent | State the exact name, logo, data and quote, plus withdrawal and use terms |
| Exit certificate | Confirm access was revoked and assets returned. State the data and configuration export or deletion status |

Do not copy a foreign template without review. Ask a Maharashtra commercial and privacy lawyer to fit it to the real business and bargaining risk.

### 31.9 Bookkeeping system

From the first paid job, use double-entry accounting software or another system approved by a CA. Keep separate ledgers for:

| Category | Examples |
|---|---|
| Revenue | Diagnostics, setup, retainers, change work, training and clear pass-through amounts |
| Cost of service | Client-specific cloud, contractor, support and QA costs only when you pay them |
| Software/admin | Email, password manager, accounting and general automation or demo tools |
| Sales/marketing | Travel, events, the website, research and referral costs |
| Professional | CA, lawyer, security review and insurance costs |
| Equipment | Laptop and accessories, using the advised asset and tax treatment |
| Taxes/statutory | GST collected or payable, TDS credits or payable, income and advance-tax reserves, and PT |
| Receivables/deferred revenue | Issued but unpaid invoices and advance receipts, as advised |
| Founder drawings/capital | Never hide a personal withdrawal as a business cost |

Attach the bill, business purpose, approver, client allocation and payment reference. Owning a business does not turn a personal cost into a business expense.

### 31.10 Monthly close checklist

1. Match every bank, account and payment-gateway balance.
2. Match invoices, receipts, credit notes and the ageing report.
3. Record vendor bills, contractor invoices and accrued costs.
4. Keep pass-through and refundable client money separate.
5. With the CA, match GST, TDS and statutory ledgers to the filing calendar.
6. Assign delivery hours and cost to each client.
7. Calculate revenue, cash gross contribution and economic margin, including founder labour.
8. Review prepaid and deferred revenue and future work you still owe.
9. Review cash runway, the tax reserve and coming annual renewals.
10. Check reliance on a few clients and overdue receivables.
11. Store records securely. Back up the accounting file.
12. Sign and date the close. Record corrections in the next period. Never overwrite a record without explanation.

### 31.11 Cash collection policy

| Work | Collection rule |
|---|---|
| Diagnostic | Collect 100% before scheduling or access |
| Pilot/setup | Prefer 60% at signature and 40% after UAT but before the live pilot. A founding pilot may require 100% in advance |
| Managed service | Bill monthly in advance, with a clear suspension and termination path |
| Change work | Collect an advance or milestone payment that matches the size of the work |
| Vendor consumption | Use the client’s card or account. Otherwise, take a prepaid, capped deposit and reconcile it clearly |
| Annual plan | Offer only after proving delivery. Fund any small discount through lower sales and administration cost |

A PO, spoken approval or sent invoice is not cash. Do not go live while a milestone is unpaid unless there is a planned and written credit decision.

### 31.12 Credit and receivable controls

- Check the legal name, GST details, address and authorised signatory;
- Put an expiry date and payment schedule in the proposal;
- Send the invoice as soon as the contract milestone occurs;
- Send polite automatic reminders before, on and after the due date;
- Review the ageing report each week;
- Stop new change work, then suspend safely under the contract. Never delete client data or hold it hostage;
- Keep export and manual fallback available during exit;
- Never give a personal kickback or pay an employee referral fee without company approval;
- Get approval for every write-off. Record it and its tax treatment.

### 31.13 Your ₹5 lakh starting-capital plan

This plan protects survival first and releases money only with evidence. Set the emergency reserve from your real essential household costs.

| Bucket | Initial cap | Purpose/release rule |
|---|---:|---|
| Personal emergency reserve | **₹3,50,000** | Keep it liquid and separate. It is not business runway or an ad budget |
| 90-day validation envelope | **₹75,000 maximum** | Release it only through the gates below. Keep any unused amount saved |
| Tax/legal/security contingency | **₹25,000** | Use it for CA or lawyer review and real risk, not general coaching |
| Hardware/health/employment buffer | **₹25,000** | Use it only when needed to keep work separate and safe |
| Uncommitted reserve | **₹25,000** | Do not assign it until paid demand creates a need |

Do not spend the full ₹50,000 monthly amount just because it is available. Keep investing or saving the unused balance under your personal financial plan.

### 31.14 Ninety-day spend gates

| Gate | Cumulative maximum | Permitted spend |
|---|---:|---|
| Before 10 interviews | ₹5,000 | Pay for local travel, and a domain or email only if needed |
| After 10 interviews and ≥3 repeat the pain | ₹15,000 | Pay for a simple landing page, demo assets and a CA consult on employment and entity choice |
| After 25 interviews and ≥5 validated accounts | ₹30,000 | Pay for synthetic demo and test tools, plus narrow legal templates or review |
| After signed paid pilot | Up to ₹75,000 | Pay for a client-specific implementation gap, insurance or professional review. Recover the cost through the fee |

Before the payment gate, never pre-buy annual SaaS, an office, employees, paid leads, mass data, a trademark package or ₹1 lakh coaching.

### 31.15 Monthly cash-waterfall rule

Apply this order to every business receipt:

```text
1. Separate collected GST/indirect tax and pass-through client money
2. With CA guidance, reserve estimated income tax and advance tax
3. Pay committed delivery, vendor and contractor costs
4. As the service grows, keep a business operating buffer of 3–6 months
5. Pay the founder only after duties and margin are clear
6. Invest any distributable surplus under the personal FIRE plan
```

Do not call collected tax, client advances for work not yet delivered or pass-through vendor deposits “profit.”

### 31.16 Client-level profitability sheet

| Line | Formula |
|---|---|
| Recognised professional revenue | Setup allocation + retainer + approved change work |
| Cash direct cost | Contractor + client-specific tools + travel + support |
| Founder delivery cost | Delivery/meeting/support/QA hours × honest replacement rate |
| Risk/rework reserve | Expected incident/scope-overrun allowance |
| Contribution | Revenue − all direct/economic delivery cost |
| Contribution margin | Contribution / revenue |
| Acquisition recovery | Contribution less CAC amortisation |
| Receivable risk | Unpaid amount × realistic collection risk |

Target a stabilised gross contribution of ≥55%, including delivery labour. A result below 50% requires standardisation, a smaller scope, a price change or exit. This is an internal business guardrail. It is not an industry statistic.

### 31.17 Annual-plan and pricing traps

- Account for currency risk on SaaS priced in euros or dollars;
- Check GST/TDS treatment and whether input credit can be recovered;
- Watch provider overages for messages, actions and seats;
- Avoid annual licences that remain unused after a client leaves;
- Include the support plan needed for production incidents;
- Plan for price increases and API deprecation;
- Include founder time in the margin;
- Never promise “unlimited” clients, projects or changes;
- Do not discount without prepayment or a smaller scope;
- Do not promise to maintain client-specific custom code forever.

### 31.18 Founder compensation and FIRE transfers

Until business cash flow is stable, do not raise your lifestyle after a spike in setup fees. Keep your salary and personal investing plan intact. Measure business profit after realistic founder labour and taxes. Distribute or invest only cash left after operating duties and reserves. If you later form a company, ask a CA about salary, dividends and director transactions. Do not move money without a clear basis.

### 31.19 Fraud and payment controls

- Send invoices only from a verified business email or system;
- Confirm bank-detail changes through a separate channel. As the team grows, require approval from two people;
- Never share an OTP, PIN or card credential with a client or vendor;
- Log and approve payments, refunds and new vendors;
- When scale allows, give proposal, bank-payment and reconciliation duties to different roles;
- Never casually route customer payments through an employee’s or personal UPI;
- Verify a high-value transfer before changing production or access;
- Keep a playbook for phishing and fake support.

### 31.20 Records calendar

Keep a calendar approved by a CA and lawyer. Cover income tax, advance tax and returns; GST invoices, returns, payments and reconciliations; TDS; PT; payroll and labour; and MCA or LLP filings where applicable. Also cover contract renewals, insurance, Udyam updates, access and security reviews and statutory retention. Rules and dates change. Subscribe to official or qualified updates, and review the calendar every quarter.

<a id="customer-success"></a>

## 32. Customer success, renewals and referrals

### 32.1 Customer-success objective

Help the client run a reliable funnel and use evidence to choose the next investment. Keep the client by giving ongoing value. Do not create dependence, hide credentials or lock in data.

### 32.2 Success plan signed at kickoff

| Section | Required decision |
|---|---|
| Business problem | Name one costly funnel failure that can be seen and measured |
| Baseline | Set the date range, counts, definitions and data limits |
| Controllable metrics | Track capture, assignment, first attempt, data completeness and the visit workflow |
| Downstream metrics | Track attendance and booking, and state the client dependencies clearly |
| Guardrails | Track opt-outs, complaints, wrong recipients, staff load, cost and reliability |
| Milestones | Set dates for the blueprint, build, UAT, limited go-live and decision review |
| Roles | Name the executive sponsor, process, CRM, site and privacy owners, and your owner |
| Adoption | State the required staff actions, training and manager schedule |
| Economic test | Set the break-even inputs and name the data owner |
| Decision | On a stated date, choose whether to convert, revise, hand off or stop |

### 32.3 Time-to-value ladder

| Time | Credible value evidence |
|---|---|
| Week 1 | Agree on the funnel dictionary. Show baseline gaps and the biggest leaks |
| Week 2 | Test source capture and routing. Make exceptions visible |
| Week 3 | Keep UAT evidence. Train users and prepare a manual fallback |
| First live week | Reconcile leads, make failures visible and have no critical-quality issue |
| 30 live days | Show comparable controllable metrics and proof of adoption |
| 60–90 days | Show stable operations, a mature visit cohort and economic sensitivity |

Do not claim an effect on later revenue before the cohort is mature.

### 32.4 Stakeholder map

| Stakeholder | Desired win | Likely fear | Evidence to show |
|---|---|---|---|
| Founder/CRO | Fewer paid leads lost and clear responsibility | Another tool no one uses | Show the funnel from baseline → current and who owns each action |
| Marketing head | Measure source quality beyond CPL | Sales may blame marketing | Follow each source cohort through an attended visit |
| Presales manager | Fair routing and fewer missed follow-ups | Surveillance and more admin work | Show a smaller queue and simpler outcomes to record |
| Sales/site head | Visits with better-prepared prospects | Poor automated bookings | Show the qualification summary and checked attendance |
| CRM/IT | A stable design that can be supported | A shadow system and security debt | Show architecture, tests, logs, rollback and client ownership |
| Finance | Predictable cost and payback | Usage or scope without limits | Show caps, margin and break-even sensitivity |
| Legal/privacy | Valid permission, true claims and controlled processors | Spam or a data breach | Show the data flow, approvals, suppression and incident plan |

### 32.5 Adoption design

Adoption is a daily process requirement. One training event is not enough.

- Ask for as few fields as possible. Use controlled one-tap outcomes;
- Show each rep how clean data reduces duplicate work and gives better context;
- Where possible, use the current CRM or inbox instead of adding another screen;
- Make the manager responsible for daily exceptions;
- Measure record completeness and age, not just login counts;
- Speak privately with users who resist the process and find the cause;
- Tell apart poor UX, lack of time, bad incentives and weak management;
- Publish change notes. Train affected roles again;
- Never use a public leaderboard to shame a person.

### 32.6 Training plan

| Audience | Content | Competency evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Rep/presales | Teach lead acceptance, attempt and contact definitions, qualification, the next action, opt-out and handoff | The user completes sample cases correctly |
| Manager | Teach the roster, routing, SLA and exception queue, overrides and the coaching report | The manager resolves an exception drill |
| Site host | Teach the visit roster, confirmation, attendance, no-show and correction process | The host completes a full visit-lifecycle drill |
| CRM/admin | Teach users, project and source mapping, the content and template process, audit and export | The admin makes a controlled change correctly |
| Privacy/legal owner | Teach consent, suppression and request and incident workflows | The owner approves test decisions |
| Executive | Teach KPI and ROI definitions, limits and the decision schedule | The executive signs the success or renewal decision |

Record attendance, the version, materials and assessment. Train new joiners. Train people again after a material change.

### 32.7 Customer health score

Give each item a score from 0–2 and keep evidence. Do not hide a red compliance score inside an average.

| Dimension | 2 = healthy | 1 = risk | 0 = critical |
|---|---|---|---|
| Executive sponsor | Attends and decides | Has delegated or is slow | Is absent or refuses decisions |
| Operational owner | Runs the queue and actions | Acts inconsistently | No owner exists |
| Data reliability | Data is defined and reconciled | Gaps can be managed | Truth cannot be measured |
| Adoption | ≥90% of agreed work is complete | 75–89% is complete | <75% is complete after correction |
| Controllable outcome | Improving or stable at a high level | Results are mixed or early | No value or realistic fix exists |
| Economics | Break-even looks possible | It is uncertain | It looks impossible or the client refuses inputs |
| Reliability/security | No unresolved high issue | Medium issues keep returning | A critical or high issue is unresolved |
| Permission/conduct | Rules are approved and followed | Coaching is needed | The client demands spam or misleading work |
| Scope/margin | Within the cap and ≥55% | Near the cap or 50–54% | The client refuses a new price or margin is <50% |
| Payment | Paid in advance or current | One late payment | Repeated delay or nonpayment |

`16–20` means healthy. `11–15` requires a recovery plan. `≤10` requires an executive decision to reduce or exit. Any critical ethics or security item can override the total score.

### 32.8 Weekly client review

1. Review decisions made since the last meeting.
2. Review data and reconciliation exceptions.
3. Review the funnel and P50/P90 response.
4. Review upcoming visits, results, no-shows and new dates.
5. Review adoption and queue age.
6. Review opt-outs, complaints and the quality sample.
7. Review changes, tests and guardrails.
8. Review scope, volume and vendor cost.
9. Give each action an owner and date.
10. State one executive blocker, if any.

### 32.9 Renewal packet

Start at least 30 days before the contract expires:

- Restate the original problem and include the signed success plan;
- Show coverage, data quality and limits;
- Show baseline and current cohort metrics with raw counts;
- Show economic sensitivity, not made-up revenue;
- Show adoption and client dependencies;
- List incidents, fixes and operating maturity;
- Show delivered scope, change hours and usage;
- State the next quarter’s bottleneck and roadmap;
- Recommend whether to continue, expand, reduce, hand off or end;
- State the price, caps, term and exit path.

### 32.10 Expansion criteria

Add a project, source or channel only after the current one passes acceptance. The client must own the data, content and coverage. Support capacity must exist. The extra work must have positive economics. Compliance and the data flow must be approved, and all relevant tests must run again. One success does not automatically prove the model in another location, project type or language.

### 32.11 Saving an at-risk account

| Root cause | Recovery action | Exit if |
|---|---|---|
| No sponsor/adoption | Reset with the executive. Simplify the workflow, name a daily owner and measure for 14 days | The owner is still absent or completion stays below the agreed minimum |
| Source quality | Show cohort evidence to marketing. Narrow the scope to facts you can control | The client demands a guarantee of buyer intent |
| Inventory/price/product | Show the bottleneck honestly. Reduce the managed scope | No value remains that you can control |
| Reliability | Pause expansion. Fix the root cause, reconcile all records and give a credit if the contract requires it | The risk stays unacceptable |
| Scope/margin | Give a usage report and propose a change or new price | The client wants work without a cap |
| Expectation gap | Compare the contract, evidence and stated non-goals | The client demands a false claim or guarantee |
| Payment | Escalate to finance and suspend safely | Late payment is repeated or becomes a default under the contract |

### 32.12 Case-study protocol

Get written permission. It must say whether to name or anonymise the client and project. It must cover the logo, exact metrics, period, denominators, screenshots and data, quote approver, publishing channels, review, expiry and withdrawal process. State any confounding factors. Also state whether results came from a vendor or an independent audit. Never turn confidential operating access into marketing material without permission.

Use this safer format:

```text
Client setting and volume range
Measured problem and baseline dates
Changes that were made
Definitions and data coverage
Before and after counts, rates and percentiles
Guardrails and incidents
Confounding factors and limits
Quote approved by the client
Date access and publication were approved
```

### 32.13 Referral engine

Ask only after a win is verified or a handoff succeeds honestly:

> “Using the agreed definitions, we measured {{specific result}}. Do you know one developer or mandate-sales team with a similar lead volume and process problem? Please introduce us only if you believe the fit is real.”

Referral fees must be clear and included in a contract. They must be invoiced, taxed and paid to the authorised business. Never pay them secretly to a client’s employee. Check partners for conflicts, anti-bribery rules and RERA limits.

### 32.14 Offboarding with dignity

| Step | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Commercial close | Record the final invoice or credit and all remaining duties |
| Operational freeze | Tell everyone when the freeze starts and who owns manual work |
| Export/handover | Deliver usable data, configuration, the runbook and current queues |
| Access | Revoke or transfer your users, tokens, webhooks and admin roles |
| Vendor/billing | Confirm the client has control. Remove your card and account |
| Open incidents/changes | Record the agreed outcome and owner |
| Data return/delete | Record completion for every system and the schedule for anything left |
| Knowledge transfer | Record attendance and proof of skill |
| Case/reference | Confirm or withdraw permission |
| Final review | Record results, limits and lessons |

Never hold client configuration or data hostage. Never damage a workflow because the retainer ends.

[Back to index](#table-of-contents)

---

<a id="founder-os"></a>

## 33. Founder time and productivity system

### 33.1 Your actual constraint

With a full-time hybrid job, your main limit is reliable attention. Money and software are not the main limits. Build a business that you can sell and run in set time blocks. Do not pretend the founder can give 24/7 support.

### 33.2 Weekly capacity budget

Start with a sustainable 12–15 hours each week. Adjust for health, family, travel to work and job duties.

| Workstream | Before first client | With 1–3 clients |
|---|---:|---:|
| Research/prospecting | Spend 5 h | Spend 3 h |
| Discovery/proposals | Spend 3 h | Spend 2 h |
| Build/delivery | Spend 3 h | Spend 5 h |
| Monitoring/support | Spend 0 | Spend 2 h |
| Learning/documentation | Spend 2 h | Spend 1.5 h |
| Finance/admin/review | Spend 1 h | Spend 1 h |
| Unplanned buffer | Keep 1 h | Keep 1.5 h |

If real client work goes over this budget for four weeks, stop selling custom work. Raise the price, simplify the work or add support coverage.

### 33.3 Time-block example

| Day | Block | Work |
|---|---|---|
| Monday | 20 min in the morning + 45 min in the evening | Handle critical operations and research prospects |
| Tuesday | 60–90 min in the evening | Run discovery or a demo, or build |
| Wednesday | 20 min in the morning + 60 min in the evening | Handle operations, proposals and follow-up |
| Thursday | 60–90 min in the evening | Build, run UAT and write documents |
| Friday | 20 min in the morning | Handle only critical operations. Do not make a risky late release |
| Saturday | 4–5 h | Work onsite, run a client workshop or implement |
| Sunday | 60–90 min | Finish the weekly report, finances and plan. Then stop |

Write down the hours when clients can reach you. Do not plan site work around hours you cannot keep.

### 33.4 Work-in-progress limits

| Item | Limit while employed |
|---|---:|
| Active new implementations | Start with 1. Allow no more than 2 after the work is standard |
| Simultaneous paid diagnostics | Allow no more than 2 |
| Stable managed clients without help | Allow 3–5, but confirm this from real hours |
| Major production releases/week | Allow 1 planned window for each client |
| New tools learned at once | Learn only 1 |
| Primary ICP | Use only 1 for the first 90 days |

### 33.5 Daily capture and next-action rule

Put every promise in one trusted task system. Record the client, result, next physical action, owner and due time. A note with no next action is only reference material. Do not run operations from memory or scattered WhatsApp stars.

### 33.6 Weekly founder review

```text
1. Check health, job and family limits
2. Check cash, the tax reserve, receivables and the 90-day spending gate
3. Check the prospect funnel and next actions
4. Check client reliability, results, risks and capacity
5. Check hours for sales, delivery, support and admin, plus the effective contribution per hour
6. Choose one limit to fix next week
7. Update the list of work to stop, delegate or automate
8. Set calendar blocks and recovery time
9. Record the decision: continue, change or kill
```

### 33.7 Learning curriculum

Learn by building a real deliverable. Do not take endless courses.

| Sequence | Competency | Proof project |
|---:|---|---|
| 1 | Learn real-estate funnel and process discovery | Map one synthetic lead journey and define its terms |
| 2 | Learn CRM records, fields, roles and workflows | Build a person, enquiry and visit model in a test tenant |
| 3 | Learn APIs, webhooks, JSON and OAuth | Build authenticated sample intake with stable IDs |
| 4 | Learn idempotency, retries and reconciliation | Test replays and outages without making duplicates |
| 5 | Learn WhatsApp, channel and compliance basics | Build an approved-context decision table and test inbox |
| 6 | Learn scheduling and state machines | Build a visit lifecycle that prevents collisions |
| 7 | Learn SQL, spreadsheets and analytics | Build a cohort funnel with raw counts and P50/P90 |
| 8 | Learn security and privacy | Build a data flow, threat model and access and retention runbook |
| 9 | Learn consultative sales and scoping | Record a practice discovery call and write a one-page paid pilot |
| 10 | Learn operations and incident response | Run a failure drill, rollback and client update |

### 33.8 Evidence repository

Keep these cleaned, reusable assets:

- Keep lessons and objections from interviews;
- Keep ICP and account research;
- Keep process and data dictionaries;
- Keep the synthetic demo and tests;
- Keep the proposal and SOW clause checklist;
- Keep architecture and workflow patterns;
- Keep lessons from incidents and postmortems;
- Keep anonymised benchmarks only when you have permission;
- Keep a register of vendor, policy and source updates;
- Keep wins and losses, plus price and margin history.

Never put live client exports into the general knowledge library.

### 33.9 Decision log

| Date | Decision | Evidence | Assumptions | Revisit trigger/date | Owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

This record stops the same debate from repeating. It also makes later hiring and handoff easier.

### 33.10 Energy and burnout controls

- Keep at least one work-free block or day each week;
- Never make a high-risk release late at night;
- Limit back-to-back client calls and plan extra travel time;
- Make emergency rules tell apart inconvenience and real harm;
- Automate an alert only when someone can act on it;
- Honestly track sleep and health strain and the cost to relationships;
- Reject clients whose required hours conflict with your job;
- Raise the price or reduce the scope before you reduce safety;
- Use paid leave for a planned go-live only if employer policy allows it. Never hide a repeated conflict.

### 33.11 Resignation gate

Do not resign because of one large setup fee or a hopeful pipeline. Require all of this minimum evidence together:

- The owner receives at least ₹1.5 lakh/month after tax from the business for 9–12 months in a row;
- Recurring revenue is high quality and few receivables are overdue;
- No client provides more than 30% of revenue, and a credible customer channel exists;
- You have 12 months of personal essential costs saved apart from business cash;
- A stable team and process cover delivery, holidays and incidents;
- You have replaced health insurance and other employer benefits;
- You have modelled tax, entity and legal duties;
- You have discussed effects on a spouse or family where relevant;
- You have a written downside plan and enough runway to find another job.

These cautious thresholds are personal guardrails. They are not financial advice for everyone.

<a id="hiring"></a>

## 34. Hiring and delegation

### 34.1 Hiring principle

Hire only after you measure a repeated limit and document the work. Do not hire a salesperson to figure out an untested offer. Do not hire a developer to build a product no one has paid for.

### 34.2 Capacity signals

| Signal | Action before hiring |
|---|---|
| Delivery hours exceed the plan | Remove custom work, standardise the service and change the price |
| Support interrupts the day job | Narrow the SLA, improve reliability and add trained support coverage |
| Founder stops selling | Delegate implementation and admin work that repeats |
| Quality falls during parallel launches | Limit WIP and name an owner for tests and releases |
| A legal, security or design specialist is needed | Hire an expert for a limited task. Do not pretend to have the skill |
| One client would pay for the full role | Avoid this dependence. Secure enough runway or a contract term, or wait |

### 34.3 Team stages

| Stage | Founder focus | First capacity |
|---|---|---|
| 0–3 clients | Sell, learn, discover, design and deliver | Use only specialist contractors |
| 4–7 stable clients | Keep selling and owning the process. Review delivery | Add an automation or implementation worker and a QA contractor or employee |
| 8–12 | Focus on enterprise sales, productisation and key results | Add a customer-success or RevOps analyst |
| 12–20 | Focus on partners, hiring, product and economics | Add a solutions lead and support or QA coverage |
| 20+ | Focus on leadership and where to use capital | Add delivery pods, a CSM, support and sales only as the economics prove the need |

### 34.4 Contractor versus employee

| Factor | Contractor | Employee |
|---|---|---|
| Work | A limited specialist or project result | An ongoing, managed core role |
| Speed/flexibility | Usually high | Usually lower at first |
| Knowledge continuity | Lower unless work is documented | Higher |
| Management/legal | Contract, security and access, tax and classification risks | Payroll, labour, benefits and management duties |
| Best early use | A lawyer, CA, security test, design task or separate integration | Stable implementation or customer-success work that repeats |

The real facts decide worker classification, not the contract label. Get employment and payroll advice.

### 34.5 First implementation hire scorecard

| Competency | Weight | Work-sample evidence |
|---|---:|---|
| Process/data modelling | 20 | Keeps contact, enquiry and visit separate and defines each state |
| Reliability | 20 | Handles idempotency, retries, partial failure and reconciliation |
| Security/privacy | 15 | Uses minimisation, access controls, secret protection and tenant isolation |
| CRM/API execution | 15 | Builds and documents an integration with supported methods |
| Testing | 15 | Tests positive, negative, concurrent and failure cases |
| Client communication | 10 | Explains trade-offs and writes a runbook |
| Judgment | 5 | Uses clear rules and human handoff instead of AI theatre |

Reject a person who asks for production passwords in chat or cannot explain protection from duplicates. Also reject anyone who uses real client data in public tools, has no fallback or promises perfect autonomous work.

### 34.6 Work-sample assignment

Give the candidate synthetic data. Ask them to design and test:

- A signed JSON intake;
- Duplicate delivery and two copies arriving at the same time;
- The same phone for enquiries about different projects;
- A missing project or phone;
- Routing by project and availability, with a fallback;
- A permitted acknowledgement and an opt-out;
- An API timeout and rate limit;
- Visit booking, rescheduling and cancellation;
- Audit, alerts, reconciliation and rollback;
- A short explanation for the client.

Pay for any work sample that has real value. Do not use a candidate’s work in production without an agreement and payment.

### 34.7 Interview questions

1. “The CRM write works, but the message request times out. What do you do?”
2. “Two events arrive in the same millisecond. How do you stop a double booking?”
3. “A salesperson tells you to delete an opt-out. What do you do?”
4. “Which data would you keep out of logs?”
5. “How would you prove that no leads were lost during an outage?”
6. “When would you use built-in CRM automation instead of n8n or custom code?”
7. “How would you hand off a client you built without creating risk?”
8. “Tell me about a failure. Which control changed because of it?”

### 34.8 Hiring gate

Require all of these:

- Show a stable workload that repeats for eight weeks;
- Have an SOP and scorecard for the role, plus manager capacity;
- Keep at least six months of the full role cost in business runway;
- Have stable MRR of at least 2× the full role cost;
- Do not let one cancellable client be the only source of funding for the role;
- Prepare employment or contractor, IP, confidentiality, payroll or tax and data-access documents;
- Prepare an equipment, account, security and offboarding plan;
- Define probation, reviews and quality measures.

### 34.9 Onboarding plan: 30 days

| Period | Scope |
|---|---|
| Days 1–3 | Teach the business, funnel, security, privacy, synthetic environment and policies |
| Days 4–7 | Let the person observe one workflow and finish the test pack |
| Week 2 | Let the person own documentation or a low-risk change, with review |
| Week 3 | Run a synthetic incident and reconciliation, plus a client role-play |
| Week 4 | Let the person deliver a limited client task with approval. Sign off competency |

Give production, admin and client access only when the work needs it and the person has proved the skill. Time in the role is not enough.

### 34.10 Delegation packet

```text
Required result and acceptance criteria
Context, client, workflow and version
Actions in scope and actions that are clearly forbidden
Inputs and data classification
Environment, access and approvers
Links to the SOP, tests and runbook
Timeline and checkpoints
Conditions that require escalation
Required evidence and output format
Rollback and closeout steps
```

### 34.11 Performance measures

Measure acceptance on the first review. Measure escaped defects and their severity. Also measure complete documents, reconciliation and incident handling. Track cycle time after quality checks, client communication and ideas for improvement. Do not reward the number of workflows, messages or closed tickets. Those rewards can cause unneeded automation and hidden failures.

### 34.12 Separation/offboarding

At once, disable the person’s email, CRM, cloud, BSP, calendar, telephony, code, password-manager and VPN accounts. Rotate any shared or exposed credentials. Transfer tickets, configuration, domain and billing. Recover the device. Check exports and downloads. Assign ownership to someone else. Restate confidentiality duties. Record deletion of client and system data. Keep the lawful audit identity. Test access after revocation.

<a id="scaling"></a>

## 35. Scaling, specialisation and productisation

### 35.1 Scale sequence

```text
repeated problem → paid pilot → accepted result → renewal
→ standard scope/SOP/tests → delivery hire → repeatable customer acquisition
→ nearby segment or location → reusable product module
```

If you skip a gate, disorder in the custom service becomes software debt.

### 35.2 Stage gates

| Stage | Focus | Exit evidence |
|---|---|---|
| 0 discovery | Focus on one Pune corridor and ICP. Run 25 interviews | ≥5 report the same costly problem, and ≥1 pays |
| 1 founder proof | Run 1–3 narrow pilots | Get a renewal, a case with numbers and a complete runbook and test pack |
| 2 repeatability | Serve 4–8 Pune/PCMC clients | Get two renewals or cases, ≥55% contribution and setup in ≤21 days, with no unresolved critical incident |
| 3 team | Delegate implementation and customer success | Keep quality and margin stable without the founder doing every step |
| 4 adjacent Maharashtra | Enter nearby cities through partners and referrals | Prove the same ICP economics and local workflow |
| 5 national | Create playbooks for selected cities and verticals | Retain 10+ clients, reach mature security and make acquisition predictable |
| 6 product/cross-border | Offer a repeated module or licensed partner model | Build a separate business case and become ready for legal, tax, support and security needs |

### 35.3 What becomes standard

| Standardise | Keep configurable |
|---|---|
| Use a standard funnel-dictionary outline | Let the client approve exact statuses |
| Use a standard contact, enquiry and visit data model | Configure project and source fields and retention |
| Use a standard intake, idempotency and retry pattern | Configure provider-specific authentication and mapping |
| Use a standard consent and suppression decision structure | Configure the client’s purpose, evidence and channel decision |
| Use a standard routing-engine framework | Configure the roster, priority, capacity and hours |
| Use a standard booking state machine | Configure site capacity, buffers and the host process |
| Use standard test, incident and change templates | Configure risk-specific cases and the SLA |
| Use standard dashboard and cohort formulas | Configure client definitions and economics |
| Use a standard schedule for onboarding and weekly reviews | Configure stakeholders and the decision schedule |

### 35.4 Productisation signals

Build reusable software only when 5–10 paying clients each need the same workflow, data model or integration. Manual delivery cost must be meaningful. A stable API path must exist. You must be able to separate and test the control. Clients must pay for ongoing ownership. Building and supporting it must cost less than repeated configuration.

Do not turn these into products:

- An exception for one client;
- Unofficial scraping or browser automation;
- A client policy that changes quickly but is stored as fixed code;
- A dashboard that cannot match the source of truth;
- AI conversation before fixed states and data work correctly;
- Features the client’s CRM already provides;
- Copied customer data used as a “benchmark moat.”

### 35.5 Moat ladder

1. Build exact knowledge of the Pune real-estate funnel.
2. Build a reusable diagnostic and baseline method.
3. Build a reliable library of connectors, states, tests and runbooks.
4. Build strong adoption and a regular manager process.
5. Build trusted case studies with honest measurements.
6. Build partnerships with CRMs, agencies and developers.
7. Build anonymised, combined benchmarks only with contract and privacy support.
8. Build narrow software modules only from repeated paid needs.

Access to tools and prompt templates alone does not protect the business from competitors.

### 35.6 Geography expansion score

Give each city a score of `0–2` for every item:

| Criterion | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Warm distribution | Partners or clients can make introductions |
| ICP density | A target-account list can be checked |
| Project economics | The buyer can afford the package |
| Workflow similarity | Current templates fit without much custom work |
| Language/local operations | Qualified people can provide coverage and review content |
| Onsite/site-visit practicality | The client or partner owns the physical work |
| Legal/tax/privacy readiness | Professional review is complete |
| Support time zone | Staff can meet the promised hours |
| Competition/differentiation | A gap in implementation or operations still exists |
| Case relevance | Current evidence applies in a believable way |

Require a score of ≥16/20 before a planned launch. Otherwise, keep the current focus.

### 35.7 Partner channels

| Partner | Mutual value | Contract boundary |
|---|---|---|
| Digital/lead-gen agency | You prove later funnel results. They improve customer acquisition | Define attribution and who owns the client |
| CRM implementer/vendor | You add real-estate workflows, adoption and managed operations | Define support, escalation and resale terms |
| Telephony/BSP | You connect response and status evidence | Define client consent, account ownership and vendor liability |
| CA/lawyer/security firm | Each side makes qualified governance referrals | Ban unlicensed advice and secret referral fees |
| CREDAI/industry network/events | Reach many prospects and teach them efficiently | Do not imply endorsement or spam members |
| BPO/call centre later | Add human coverage | Define training, scripts, employment, privacy, quality and legal responsibility |

### 35.8 White-label decision

Offer a white-label service only with written rules. Cover who owns each account, plus brand and customer messages. Cover data roles, subprocessors, sales promises, prices and payment. Add support escalation, IP, configuration and case studies. Also cover non-circumvention where lawful and suitable, incident notice and exit. Reject any deal that lets an agency promise unlimited or illegal results while you carry hidden delivery risk.

### 35.9 Packaging at scale

| Package | Standard cap idea | Value |
|---|---|---|
| Diagnostic | Limit it to one project and approved, combined data for 6–8 weeks | Give a baseline, leaks, economics and a roadmap |
| Core | Limit it to one project, ≤3 sources, ≤750 leads/month and ≤10 users | Give reliable capture, routing, response, visits and reports |
| Growth | Limit it to ≤3 projects, ≤6 sources, ≤2,500 leads and ≤25 users | Give a workflow for several teams, a weekly review and deeper analysis |
| Scale | Cover several teams or cities, with custom security, BI or support | Design it for the client with a delivery team |

These limits are business guesses. Measure the real effort and update the package and version. Never promise unlimited work forever to an existing client.

### 35.10 Scaling metrics

| Dimension | Measure |
|---|---|
| Sales | Track the qualified pipeline, win rate, CAC and payback, and the sales cycle by ICP and channel |
| Onboarding | Track days from access-ready → go-live, UAT passed on the first try and implementation hours |
| Delivery | Track hours per client, exceptions, reliability, changes and support load |
| Outcomes | Track metrics the client can control, renewals, expansion and case quality |
| Economics | Track contribution margin, cash conversion, DSO, client concentration and runway |
| People | Track capacity and use, quality, time to gain skill and attrition |
| Productisation | Track the share reused, custom branches and defect rate for each module |
| Risk | Track incidents and severity, privacy and security actions, and overdue controls |

### 35.11 Cross-border expansion warning

A foreign opportunity does not justify calling consumers or moving data too early. Before entering the UAE or any other market, first prove the service in India. Require a locally licensed client or partner. Get qualified local and Indian advice about real-estate advertising, telemarketing, privacy, tax and permanent establishment. Use client-owned accounts, staffed support, language review, contract and currency-risk controls, and separate test and data controls. Start with remote implementation for a licensed business. Do not start with cold outreach to consumers.

### 35.12 When to stay a profitable service

A focused consultancy with 5–15 clients and high margins may reach the founder’s money goal faster and with less risk than venture-style SaaS. Fund a product only when repeated demand and the economics support it. A product is not a required next step.

<a id="risk-register"></a>

## 36. Risk register

### 36.1 Scoring method

Use `Priority /100 = likelihood (1–5) × impact (1–5) × 4`. Likelihood means how likely it is. Impact means how much harm it could cause. This score helps you decide what to handle first; it is not a true probability. Score every risk again each month and after an important change. A legal or ethical stop rule always wins over the score.

| ID | Risk | L | I | Priority /100 | Early signal | Prevent/mitigate | Kill/escalate trigger |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| R01 | RERA may treat you as an agent, but you are not registered | 4 | 5 | **80** | Your work includes replying to buyers, qualifying them, introducing them or booking visits | Get a written Maharashtra opinion. Stay backend-only, or complete registration, certification and project association | Do not contact a live buyer until the model is cleared |
| R02 | Conflict with your employer, moonlighting rule or IP ownership | 3 | 5 | **60** | Same or related industry, exclusivity, NOC or IP clause, or support needed in job hours | Review the agreement and policy. Get a written NOC if needed. Keep time, device, code and data separate | Stop until the employer or lawyer clears it |
| R03 | The client cannot prove that a lead was lawfully obtained | 4 | 5 | **80** | A bought sheet, or no form, consent proof or version | Require source evidence and a client promise in the contract. Start with a small checked group | Reject the list or client if its origin cannot be proved |
| R04 | WhatsApp or TRAI breach, or the channel gets banned | 4 | 5 | **80** | Personal SIM, bulk tool, bypassed template or complaints | Use the client's WABA, DLT and registered route. Check opt-in, suppression, limits and policy | Stop that channel if someone bypasses controls or a quality warning appears |
| R05 | A false or old project, price or inventory claim | 3 | 5 | **60** | Old brochure, spoken updates only or fake shortage | Use an approved source with version and expiry. Require a person to confirm it | Turn off that content or project until it is corrected |
| R06 | Data goes to the wrong person or crosses between clients | 3 | 5 | **60** | Shared client setup or unclear identity | Separate clients, give least access, check recipients, and use a canary, tests and kill switch | Treat as SEV-1 and stop the affected action at once |
| R07 | A lead is lost because the workflow fails silently | 4 | 4 | **64** | Source and CRM counts differ, or no recent events arrive | Keep a durable receipt, idempotency, exception queue, record comparison and fallback | Do not expand. Use manual continuity until every gap is explained |
| R08 | Duplicate messages or bookings | 3 | 4 | **48** | Provider retries, or CRM and calendar disagree | Use stable IDs, an atomic upsert or lock, saved send intent and replay tests | Turn off sending or booking. Make records agree before replay |
| R09 | No person at the client can handle leads | 4 | 4 | **64** | Leads become old even though the system is fast | Make staff, roster and SLA a start condition. Name a fallback manager | Do not launch or renew without human coverage |
| R10 | Staff do not use the CRM | 4 | 4 | **64** | Hidden spreadsheets, missing results or next actions | Make fields simpler. Use a manager routine, training and visible queues | If use stays <80% after a correction period, reset or leave |
| R11 | Leads are poor or the property does not fit | 4 | 3 | **48** | Fast attempts but low contact or qualification | Group results by source and give feedback to marketing | Escalate if the client expects automation to create buyer intent |
| R12 | No buyer with budget or no urgent problem | 4 | 3 | **48** | Meetings only with junior staff and vague comments such as “interesting” | Apply the ICP, sponsor and payment gates | Do not build a custom solution or proposal without an owner and real pain |
| R13 | A false claim about which change caused ROI | 3 | 5 | **60** | Many changes at once or meanings keep changing | Use a baseline, fixed groups, a log of other changes and sensitivity ranges | Refuse a cause-and-effect guarantee or a changed denominator |
| R14 | Too little volume or value to make the work pay | 3 | 4 | **48** | Extra visits needed to break even are more than total possible visits | Use the `VISIT-READY` score and a range for contribution | Sell only a paid diagnostic, or reject the work |
| R15 | Scope keeps growing and every client gets custom work | 5 | 4 | **80** | Extra projects, sources or support requested in chat | Set limits, use change orders and a versioned backlog, and review margin | Raise the price, reduce scope or leave if margin is below 50% |
| R16 | Price is too low because your work time was ignored | 4 | 4 | **64** | Revenue looks high, but time and cash are missing and support is tiring | Track hours, include replacement cost, and set setup and retainer minimums | Stop selling until you fix the package |
| R17 | Client does not pay, causing a cash gap | 3 | 4 | **48** | Deposit is late or client asks you to pay for tools | Bill early, make client pay vendors and keep a right to suspend work | Do not do live work before the milestone payment |
| R18 | Founder burnout, poor health or family harm | 4 | 4 | **64** | Night incidents, lost sleep or missed job duties | Limit capacity and work in progress. Set a bounded SLA and recovery time, or hire help | Stop onboarding or reduce scope |
| R19 | Too much revenue depends on one client | 3 | 4 | **48** | One client is >30% of revenue or pays for a new hire | Add other clients, keep runway and limit fixed costs | Do not quit or hire based on one client who can cancel |
| R20 | Vendor outage, API change or old version ends | 4 | 3 | **48** | Errors, renewal or version notice, or expired webhook | Keep a tool register, monitoring, queue and fallback. Review every quarter | Turn off the unsafe path and move vendors through a change plan |
| R21 | Account or secret is stolen | 3 | 5 | **60** | New login, admin or export, or token appears in chat or logs | Use MFA, a vault, rotation, access alerts and proper offboarding | Revoke and rotate it, then start the incident process at once |
| R22 | AI invents a fact or takes an unsafe action | 3 | 5 | **60** | Unsupported fact, uncertain intent or prompt injection | Use rules first, approved retrieval, fixed validation, a human path and evaluations | Remove AI until all repeat tests pass |
| R23 | Data cannot be found, deleted or handled for a rights request | 3 | 4 | **48** | Nobody can locate or remove copies and exports | Keep a data map, few copies, automatic retention work and request drills | Pause new data use if the control does not exist |
| R24 | A required incident report is late | 2 | 5 | **40** | No PoC, correct clock, timeline or legal owner | Keep CERT-In and DPDP playbooks and run a tabletop drill. Use a one-hour vendor → client target | Escalate at once. Do not wait until you are certain |
| R25 | You unexpectedly become a PMLA reporting entity | 2 | 5 | **40** | Services relate to a real-estate sale or purchase and yearly turnover approaches ₹20L. RERA status is one sign, but not the PMLA test | Get a separate G.S.R. 798(E) classification. Run an AML review at ₹15L annualised turnover. Prepare FINnet, officer, CDD, reporting and record controls | Do not cross the threshold without an opinion and readiness |
| R26 | Tax, GST or foreign-service treatment is wrong | 3 | 4 | **48** | ₹20L threshold, UAE client, foreign SaaS or commission | Get a CA memo, track turnover monthly, and keep correct invoices and books | Pause that invoice or structure until it is classified |
| R27 | A partner or white-label seller acts badly | 3 | 4 | **48** | Partner overpromises, spams or hides the real client | Control scope, data, promises and incidents directly. Keep a right to audit | End illegal or misleading activity |
| R28 | Unofficial integration or scraping | 3 | 4 | **48** | Browser extension, session cookie or no supported API | Use an official connector or an approved manual fallback | Reject the workaround |
| R29 | The physical site is not ready | 3 | 3 | **36** | Wrong map, closed site or absent host | Check the real location, roster and disruption process | Stop confirmations until the site facts are correct |
| R30 | SaaS or UAE expansion starts too early | 3 | 4 | **48** | Building before renewals or cold-calling abroad | Use stage gates, proof in India and a local lawyer or partner | Freeze expansion when a required condition is missing |

### 36.2 Risk review ritual

For each of the top five risks, name an owner. Record the next prevention action and date, the early warning sign, the score after controls, and a link to evidence. Close a risk only when the exposure is gone. Finishing one task may lower the risk without removing it.

### 36.3 Assumption register

| Assumption | Evidence | Consequence if false | Test/date | Decision owner |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Example: client has ≥300 opted-in inbound leads/month | Waiting for export or source report | Pilot may be too small and the economics may fail | Check during the diagnostic before the proposal | Sponsor |

An untested assumption that could cause major harm is a risk. Put it in this register. Never present it as a fact.

### 36.4 Business continuity priorities

| Priority | Maximum tolerable ambiguity | Continuity action |
|---|---|---|
| Stop contact or disclosure that is not allowed | None | Turn off the outgoing action and apply suppression |
| Keep a legitimate incoming lead event | Very low | Use a durable queue or manual source export |
| Keep a visit promise accurate | Very low | Site coordinator checks it and compares the calendar by hand |
| Restore the owner and tasks | A short, agreed delay | Manager queue and manual assignment |
| Restore dashboards and nurture | A longer delay is acceptable | Pause them until the real operating records agree |

### 36.5 Insurance discussion

As contracts and data risk grow, ask an insurance broker and lawyer about suitable cover. Ask about professional indemnity or errors-and-omissions cover. Also ask about cyber and privacy incidents, commercial general liability, and employee or fidelity cover. Read every exclusion. Pay special attention to work without a licence, liability accepted in a contract, fines and penalties, deliberate acts, old incidents, work across countries, subcontractors and social engineering. Insurance never gives you permission to ignore controls.

<a id="troubleshooting"></a>

## 37. Troubleshooting playbooks

### 37.1 Diagnostic discipline

Before changing anything:

1. State what you can see, its effect, when it began and what it affects.
2. Protect the evidence. Save correlation, event, lead, visit and provider IDs, plus versions.
3. Check whether any customer contact, booking or security action is unsafe. If yes, contain it first.
4. Compare the official systems. Do not trust only one dashboard.
5. When safe, recreate the problem with a made-up test record.
6. Test one idea that can be proved wrong at a time.
7. Change only the smallest controlled part.
8. Run repeat tests, make records agree and write down the result.
9. Roll back the change if a safety measure gets worse.

### 37.2 Symptom-to-test matrix

| Symptom | First tests | Common causes | Corrective path |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source shows more leads than CRM | Use the same period, time zone and unique IDs. Check rejected and held items and the latest webhook | Provider did not deliver, authorisation or data shape is wrong, mapping failed, or queue failed | Restore intake, get a source export, import safely with idempotency, and make records agree |
| CRM has duplicates | Compare source event IDs and the design for people versus enquiries | Replay was not controlled, two actions happened at once, or a phone number was used as an enquiry ID | Claim each event once in the ledger and upsert. Merge people only after review, and keep all events |
| Leads have no owner | Check roster, rules, version, fallback and API permissions | No rep is eligible, first matching rule is wrong, capacity is full, or owner is disabled | Use the monitored fallback. Fix master data or rules and replay only the assignments |
| Response feels “slow” | Measure receive, CRM, assignment, system acknowledgement, human attempt and contact separately | No human coverage, a queue, wrong business-hours meaning or provider delay | Fix the exact blockage. Do not hide it with an automatic message |
| Contact rate is poor | Check each source, phone validity, time, caller name and attempts by group | Poor source, DND, wrong numbers or rep behaviour | Give evidence to marketing or the phone owner. Follow people's preferences; do not send more spam |
| Qualification falls | Check where people leave each question, property fit, language and a sample by rep | Too many questions, wrong project, or a problem in script or data | Ask fewer or branching questions, offer a person, and give product feedback to the client |
| Many visits booked, few attended | Check meanings, results, reschedules, map and host | Bookings were not real, slots or directions were bad, intent was weak, or site had a problem | Improve confirmation, rescheduling, directions and host work. Split results by site, slot and source |
| Visits missing from calendar | Compare visit ID, calendar mapping, webhook and sync channel | Part of the workflow failed, someone deleted it, or the watch expired | Stop false confirmations. Make records agree or recreate safely with idempotency, and renew sync |
| Calendar has two visits | Search for the fixed IDs and retries that created events | Timeout result was unclear, retry was blind, or two actions happened at once | Coordinator reviews and keeps one. Add a fixed ID or lock |
| Reminder sent after cancellation | Check queue or job cancellation and when state was read | Old cached state, systems not connected or two actions happened at once | Stop the queue, check all affected contacts and read current state again just before sending |
| WhatsApp template rejected or paused | Read the provider's reason, category, language and current policy | Wrong variables or content, quality issue or policy change | Use an approved fallback. Fix, approve and test again. Never bypass it with free-form text |
| WhatsApp failures suddenly rise | Check provider, account, quality, rate, number and source status | Account limit, bad numbers, outage or complaints | Slow or stop sending. Check provider status and support, and have client review compliance |
| SMS not delivered | Check PE, header, template, registered chain, scrub and provider status | DLT does not match, DND applies or variable does not match | Client, TSP or RTM fixes it. Never move to a personal SIM |
| AI gives a wrong fact | Check source, version, retrieval, prompt and output-validation logs | Old knowledge, unsupported generation or injection | Turn off the AI answer, send to a person, fix the source and control, and repeat all evaluations |
| AI misses an opt-out | Check the exact words, language, classifier and limit | Keyword list is too narrow or language is mixed | Use strict fixed rules plus a person. Add reviewed cases to the critical test set |
| Dashboard total does not match | Check query version, time, group, exclusions, joins and duplicate removal | Many-to-many join, reschedules or a state that changed | Compare raw IDs, version the KPI and correct old results if needed |
| Funnel rate suddenly “improves” | Check raw denominators, validity rules and state changes | Records were reclassified, a source is missing or recent group is not mature | Mark the break in measurement. Run the comparison again with matching meanings |
| Workflow cost suddenly rises | Split volume by workflow and inspect retries, loops and message jobs | Retry storm, one paid action for every item, or bad trigger | Use a circuit breaker and cost limit. Redesign batching and state, and review the vendor plan |
| Client says “leads are bad” | Check validity, contact, fit and attendance for each source | Marketing quality or the sales process | Show evidence for each stage, agree the owner and avoid one broad conclusion |
| Reps avoid the CRM | Check too many fields, mobile use, incentives and whether managers use it | Process does not fit or sponsor is weak | Remove fields, connect the tools, coach users and give someone daily ownership of exceptions |
| Client asks for endless changes | Check scope, use, change log and margin | SOW is unclear or chat requests are treated as free | Group the backlog, use a change order, raise the price or refuse |
| Retainer loses money | Check hours, costs, incidents and scope for that client | Founder time was ignored or too many custom paths exist | Standardise, raise the price, reduce work or leave |
| Nobody replies to outreach | Compare ICP, list, trigger, message and delivery by group | Generic message, wrong buyer, no proof or no painful problem | Run 10 customer interviews, narrow the target and change one thing at a time |
| Discovery calls do not become paid pilots | Review recorded objections, authority, economics, data and access | Curiosity without pain, too much free advice or risk is too high | Sell a paid diagnostic, make conditions and proof stronger, or reject the lead |

### 37.3 Reconciliation repair rules

| Gap | Safe repair |
|---|---|
| Source event missing in CRM | Check current consent, project and state again. Import with the first ID and time. Block any old action that should no longer happen |
| CRM lead has no message | Check current eligibility and conversation, plus the provider's existing send intent and status. Do not resend automatically |
| Message exists but CRM activity is missing | Add the provider ID and status to the existing lead. Do not send the customer another message |
| CRM visit has no calendar event | Check what the customer and site actually agreed. Create an event with a fixed ID, or mark an exception before communicating |
| Calendar visit has no CRM record | Search by the private opaque visit ID, owner and time. Create or link only after checking identity |
| Opt-out differs between systems | Apply the safest suppression at once. Then find its proper scope and evidence |
| Owner differs between tools | Keep the history. Client manager chooses the official current owner. Stop update loops |

### 37.4 Bad-data correction

Never change live data directly without a ticket and an authorised person. Keep the before and after record, source evidence, affected later states, repeat-test results and proof that systems agree. A bulk correction also needs a dry-run count, backup or export, second approval, small canary and rollback.

### 37.5 “No improvement” diagnosis order

```text
definitions/data comparable?
→ enough volume/maturation?
→ workflow technically executed?
→ client staff adopted it?
→ targeted stage improved?
→ next bottleneck shifted?
→ source/product/price/inventory/confounder changed?
→ economics still plausible?
→ continue one experiment, reduce scope or exit
```

Do not treat AI as the automatic answer to this problem.

### 37.6 Escalation packet to a vendor/client admin

Include the non-secret account or tenant name, times and time zone. Add the endpoint, API and version, plus a safe request or correlation ID. Give the response or error code and the smallest way to recreate the problem. State the expected result, actual result, effect and count. Add retries, containment and permission for support access. Hide tokens and customer data that support does not need.

### 37.7 When to stop troubleshooting

Escalate or leave when the client will not provide official data, needed access or an owner. Also stop when a vendor path is not supported. Stop if continuing could cause illegal contact or data loss. Escalate if the same root cause returns because the client will not act, or if the scope cannot make money. Get a qualified lawyer, CA or security professional when the issue needs one. Being persistent does not mean acting without authority.
